# COVID-19

This article is about the disease itself. For the virus that causes the disease, see [SARS-CoV-2](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/SARS-CoV-2). For the global pandemic caused by the disease, see [COVID-19 pandemic](62750956.md). For other diseases caused by coronaviruses, see [Coronavirus diseases](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Coronavirus_diseases).

**Coronavirus disease 2019** (**COVID-19**) is a [contagious disease](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Contagious_disease) caused by the [coronavirus](201983.md) [SARS-CoV-2](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/SARS-CoV-2). Starting in January 2020, the disease spread worldwide, resulting in the [COVID-19 pandemic](62750956.md). In March 2020, the [World Health Organization](33583.md) declared COVID-19 a [global health emergency](42694003.md); they declared the end of the emergency in May 2023.[^cite_note-7]

The [symptoms of COVID‑19](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Symptoms_of_COVID%E2%80%9119) can vary but often include fever,[^cite_note-8] fatigue, cough, [shortness of breath](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shortness_of_breath), [loss of smell](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anosmia), and [loss of taste](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ageusia).[^cite_note-9][^cite_note-10][^cite_note-11] Symptoms may begin one to fourteen days [after exposure](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Incubation_period) to the virus. At least a third of people who are infected [do not develop noticeable symptoms](602790.md).[^cite_note-12][^cite_note-13] Of those who develop symptoms noticeable enough to be classified as patients, most (81%) develop mild to moderate symptoms (up to mild [pneumonia](52135.md)), while 14% develop severe symptoms ([dyspnea](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dyspnea), [hypoxia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hypoxia_\(medical\)), or more than 50% lung involvement on imaging), and 5% develop critical symptoms ([respiratory failure](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Respiratory_failure), [shock](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shock_\(circulatory\)), or [multiorgan dysfunction](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Organ_dysfunction)).[^cite_note-CDC_Interim_Guidance-14] Older people have a higher risk of developing severe symptoms and dying. Some people experience persistent symptoms ([long COVID](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Long_COVID)), for months or years after infection, including [fatigue](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fatigue), cognitive issues and shortness of breath. Damage to organs has been observed in a subset.[^cite_note-davis-15]

[COVID‑19 transmission](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Transmission_of_COVID-19) occurs when infectious particles are breathed in or come into contact with the eyes, nose, or mouth. The risk is highest when people are close together, but small [airborne](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Airborne_transmission) particles containing the virus can remain suspended in the air and travel over longer distances, particularly indoors. Transmission can also occur when people touch their eyes, nose, or mouth after touching surfaces or objects that have been contaminated by the virus. People remain contagious for up to 20 days and can spread the virus even if they do not develop symptoms.[^cite_note-16]

There are two common [tests to detect a COVID infection](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/COVID-19_testing). Antigen tests (also called [rapid lateral flow tests](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/COVID-19_rapid_antigen_test)) can be used at home. A positive test indicates an active infection. However, negative test results are not always accurate, especially when there are no symptoms.[^cite_note-\:6-17] Health care providers can perform a more accurate [PCR test](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/PCR_test), which is typically analysed in a laboratory.[^cite_note-18][^cite_note-\:6-17]

Several [COVID-19 vaccines](63319438.md) have been approved and distributed in various countries, many of which have initiated [mass vaccination campaigns](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Deployment_of_COVID-19_vaccines). Other [preventive measures](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pandemic_prevention) include physical or [social distancing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Social_distancing), [quarantining](25237.md), ventilation of indoor spaces, [use of face masks or coverings](63631542.md) in public, covering coughs and sneezes, [hand washing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hand_washing), and keeping unwashed hands away from the face. Initial [treatment](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Treatment_and_management_of_COVID-19) consists of [drugs that have been developed](63435931.md) to inhibit the virus for those at high risk and [symptomatic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Symptomatic_treatment) treatment, managing the disease through supportive care.

The first known case was [identified in Wuhan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/COVID-19_pandemic_in_Hubei), China, in December 2019.[^cite_note-WSJ-20210226-19] Most scientists believe that the SARS-CoV-2 virus entered into human populations through natural [zoonosis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Zoonosis), similar to the [SARS-CoV-1](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/SARS-CoV-1) and [MERS-CoV](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/MERS-CoV) outbreaks, and consistent with other pandemics in human history.[^cite_note-pekar-20][^cite_note-jiang_wang-21] Social and environmental factors including [climate change](5042951.md), [natural ecosystem destruction](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Environmental_degradation) and [wildlife trade](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wildlife_trade_and_zoonoses) increased the likelihood of such zoonotic spillover.[^cite_note-IPCC-2022a-22][^cite_note-IPCC-2022b-23][^cite_note-University-of-Cambridge-2021-24][^cite_note-European-Commission-25]

## Nomenclature

Main article: [COVID-19 naming](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/COVID-19_naming)

During the initial outbreak in [Wuhan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wuhan), the virus was commonly called "coronavirus" and "Wuhan coronavirus",[^cite_note-26][^cite_note-27][^cite_note-28] and the disease was referred to by the same terms and sometimes as "Wuhan pneumonia".[^cite_note-29][^cite_note-30] In the past, many diseases have been named after geographical locations, such as the [Spanish flu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Spanish_flu),[^cite_note-31] [Japanese encephalitis](1432191.md), [Middle East respiratory syndrome](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Middle_East_respiratory_syndrome), and [Zika virus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Zika_virus).[^cite_note-Nature_Stop-32] In January 2020, the [World Health Organization](33583.md) (WHO) recommended 2019-nCoV[^cite_note-33] and 2019-nCoV acute respiratory disease[^cite_note-34] as interim names for the virus and disease per 2015 guidance and international guidelines against using geographical locations or groups of people in disease and virus names to prevent [social stigma](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Social_stigma).[^cite_note-35][^cite_note-veconomist-36][^cite_note-37] The official names COVID‑19 and SARS-CoV-2 were issued by the WHO on 11 February 2020 with COVID-19 being shorthand for "coronavirus disease 2019".[^cite_note-WHO-naming-38][^cite_note-39] The WHO additionally uses "the COVID‑19 virus" and "the virus responsible for COVID‑19" in public communications.[^cite_note-WHO-naming-38][^cite_note-40]

## Signs and symptoms

Main article: [Symptoms of COVID-19](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Symptoms_of_COVID-19)



[Symptoms](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Signs_and_symptoms) of COVID-19

The [symptoms of COVID-19](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Symptoms_of_COVID-19) are variable depending on the [type of variant](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/COVID_variant) contracted, ranging from mild [symptoms](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Symptom) to a potentially fatal illness.[^cite_note-Symptoms_of_COVID-19_CDC2020Sym-41][^cite_note-Symptoms_of_COVID-19_Grant_et_al_2020-42] Common symptoms include [coughing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cough), [fever](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fever), [loss of smell](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anosmia) and [taste](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ageusia), with less common ones including [headaches](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Headache), [nasal congestion](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nasal_congestion) and [runny nose](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rhinorrhea), [muscle pain](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Muscle_pain), [sore throat](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sore_throat), [diarrhea](53951.md), [eye irritation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eye_irritation),[^cite_note-43] and toes swelling or turning purple,[^cite_note-44] and in moderate to severe cases, [breathing difficulties](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Breathing_difficulties).[^cite_note-Symptoms_of_COVID-19_ECDC-45] People with the COVID-19 infection may have different symptoms, and their symptoms may change over time.

Three common clusters of symptoms have been identified: a respiratory symptom cluster with cough, [sputum](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sputum), [shortness of breath](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shortness_of_breath), and fever; a musculoskeletal symptom cluster with [muscle](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Skeletal_muscle) and joint pain, headache, and fatigue; and a cluster of digestive symptoms with abdominal pain, vomiting, and diarrhea.[^cite_note-Symptoms_of_COVID-19_ECDC-45] In people without prior ear, nose, or throat disorders, [loss of taste](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hypogeusia) combined with [loss of smell](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hyposmia) is associated with COVID-19 and is reported in as many as 88% of symptomatic cases.[^cite_note-Symptoms_of_COVID-19_Paderno_2020-46][^cite_note-Symptoms_of_COVID-19_Chabot_2021-47][^cite_note-Symptoms_of_COVID-19_pmid32483687-48]

Of those who show symptoms, 81% develop only mild to moderate symptoms (up to mild [pneumonia](52135.md)), while 14% develop severe symptoms ([dyspnea](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dyspnea), [hypoxia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hypoxia_\(medical\)), or more than 50% lung involvement on imaging) that require hospitalization, and 5% of patients develop critical symptoms ([respiratory failure](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Respiratory_failure), septic [shock](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shock_\(circulatory\)), or [multiorgan dysfunction](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Organ_dysfunction)) requiring ICU admission. As of 2024–2025, the majority of infections are characterized as mild or asymptomatic; however, risk remains stratified by age and underlying health conditions, with severe outcomes—including respiratory failure and multi-organ dysfunction—occurring in a significantly smaller percentage of the general vaccinated population than in early 2020.[^cite_note-49][^cite_note-50][^cite_note-Symptoms_of_COVID-19_CDC_Interim_Guidance-51][^cite_note-52]



Proportion of asymptomatic SARS-CoV-2 infection by age. About 44% of those infected with SARS-CoV-2 remained asymptomatic throughout the infection.

[^cite_note-Symptoms_of_COVID-19_Wang2023-53]

At least a third of the people who are infected with the virus do not develop noticeable symptoms at any point in time.[^cite_note-Symptoms_of_COVID-19_Wang2023-53][^cite_note-54][^cite_note-Symptoms_of_COVID-19_Asymptomatic-55] These [asymptomatic](602790.md) carriers tend not to get tested and can still spread the disease.[^cite_note-Symptoms_of_COVID-19_Asymptomatic-55][^cite_note-56][^cite_note-57][^cite_note-Symptoms_of_COVID-19_Furukawa_2020-58] Other infected people will develop symptoms later (called "pre-symptomatic") or have very mild symptoms and can also spread the virus.[^cite_note-Symptoms_of_COVID-19_Furukawa_2020-58]

As is common with infections, there is a delay, or [incubation period](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Incubation_period), between the moment a person first becomes infected and the appearance of the first symptoms. The [median](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Median) delay for COVID-19 is four to five days[^cite_note-Symptoms_of_COVID-19_pmid32329974-59] possibly being infectious on 1–4 of those days.[^cite_note-60] Most symptomatic people experience symptoms within two to seven days after exposure, and almost all will experience at least one symptom within 12 days.[^cite_note-Symptoms_of_COVID-19_pmid32329974-59][^cite_note-61]

Most people recover from the [acute](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Acute_\(medicine\)) phase of the disease. However, some people continue to experience a range of effects, such as [fatigue](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fatigue), for prolonged periods after an initial COVID-19 infection.[^cite_note-Symptoms_of_COVID-19_cdc24-62] This is the result of a condition called [long COVID](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Long_COVID), which can be described as a range of persistent symptoms that continue for months or years.[^cite_note-Symptoms_of_COVID-19_cdc24-62] Long-term damage to organs has been observed after the onset of COVID-19. Multi-year studies are underway to further investigate the protracted effects of long COVID.[^cite_note-Symptoms_of_COVID-19_cdc24-62] Reducing the risk of long COVID includes staying up to date on the most recent COVID-19 vaccine, practicing good hygiene, maintaining clean indoor air, and physical distancing from people infected with a respiratory virus.[^cite_note-Symptoms_of_COVID-19_cdc24-62]

The [Omicron variant](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/SARS-CoV-2_Omicron_variant) became dominant in the U.S. in December 2021. Symptoms with the Omicron variant are less severe than they are with other variants.[^cite_note-63]

### Complications



Mechanisms of SARS-CoV-2 [cytokine storm](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cytokine_storm) and complications

Complications may include [pneumonia](52135.md), [acute respiratory distress syndrome](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Acute_respiratory_distress_syndrome) (ARDS), [multi-organ failure](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Multiple_organ_dysfunction_syndrome), [septic shock](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Septic_shock), and death.[^cite_note-Hui14Jan2020-64][^cite_note-65][^cite_note-StatPearls-66][^cite_note-Heymann_Shindo_2020_pp._542–545222-67] Cardiovascular complications may include heart failure, [arrhythmias](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arrhythmia) (including [atrial fibrillation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Atrial_fibrillation)), [heart inflammation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Myocarditis), [thrombosis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Thrombosis), particularly [venous thromboembolism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Venous_thromboembolism),[^cite_note-68][^cite_note-69][^cite_note-Long-2020-70][^cite_note-71][^cite_note-72][^cite_note-73] and endothelial cell injury and dysfunction.[^cite_note-74] Approximately 20–30% of people who present with COVID‑19 have [elevated liver enzymes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Elevated_transaminases), reflecting liver injury.[^cite_note-75][^cite_note-Sanders202022-76]

Neurological manifestations include [seizure](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Seizure), stroke, [encephalitis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Encephalitis), and [Guillain–Barré syndrome](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Guillain%E2%80%93Barr%C3%A9_syndrome) (which includes [loss of motor functions](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hemiparesis)).[^cite_note-Carod-Artal-2020-77][^cite_note-78] Following the infection, children may develop [paediatric multisystem inflammatory syndrome](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paediatric_multisystem_inflammatory_syndrome), which has symptoms similar to [Kawasaki disease](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kawasaki_disease), which can be fatal.[^cite_note-79][^cite_note-80] In very rare cases, acute [encephalopathy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Encephalopathy) can occur, and it can be considered in those who have been diagnosed with COVID‑19 and have an altered mental status.[^cite_note-81]

According to the US [Centers for Disease Control and Prevention](6811.md), pregnant women are at increased risk of becoming seriously ill from COVID‑19.[^cite_note-Cordoba-Vives-2020-82] This is because pregnant women with COVID‑19 appear to be more likely to develop respiratory and obstetric complications that can lead to [miscarriage](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Miscarriage), [premature delivery](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Premature_delivery) and [intrauterine growth restriction](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Intrauterine_growth_restriction).[^cite_note-Cordoba-Vives-2020-82]

Fungal infections such as [aspergillosis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Aspergillosis), [candidiasis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Candidiasis), [cryptococcosis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cryptococcosis) and [mucormycosis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mucormycosis) have been recorded in people recovering from COVID‑19.[^cite_note-83][^cite_note-84]

## Cause

COVID‑19 is caused by infection with a [coronavirus](201983.md) known as "severe acute respiratory syndrome coronavirus 2" ([SARS-CoV-2](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/SARS-CoV-2)).[^cite_note-85]

### Transmission

Main article: [Transmission of COVID-19](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Transmission_of_COVID-19)



[Transmission](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Transmission_\(medicine\)) of COVID‑19

COVID-19 is mainly transmitted from person to person through inhaling air contaminated by [droplets](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Drop_\(liquid\))/[aerosols](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Aerosol) and small [airborne](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Airborne_transmission) particles containing [the virus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/SARS-CoV-2). Infected people exhale those particles as they breathe, talk, cough, sneeze, or sing.[^cite_note-Transmission_of_COVID-19_Wang_2021-86][^cite_note-Transmission_of_COVID-19_Greenhalgh_2021-87][^cite_note-Transmission_of_COVID-19_AR2021-88][^cite_note-89] Transmission is most likely at closer range but can also occur over longer distances, particularly indoors.[^cite_note-Transmission_of_COVID-19_Wang_2021-86][^cite_note-Transmission_of_COVID-19_Miller_2021-90]

The virus spreads through virus-laden fluid particles, or droplets, which are created in the respiratory tract, and they are expelled by the mouth and the nose. There are three types of transmission: "droplet" and "contact", which are associated with large droplets, and "airborne", which is associated with small droplets.[^cite_note-Transmission_of_COVID-19_\:1-91] If the droplets are above a certain critical size, they settle faster than they [evaporate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Evaporate), and therefore they [contaminate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Contaminate) surfaces surrounding them.[^cite_note-Transmission_of_COVID-19_\:1-91] Droplets that are below a certain critical size, generally thought to be <100 [micrometres](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Micrometre) (μm) diameter, evaporate faster than they settle; due to that fact, they form respiratory aerosol particles that remain airborne for a long period of time over extensive distances.[^cite_note-Transmission_of_COVID-19_\:1-91][^cite_note-Transmission_of_COVID-19_Wang_2021-86]

[Infectivity](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Infectivity) can begin four to five days before the onset of symptoms.[^cite_note-Transmission_of_COVID-19_HeCorrection-92] Infected people can spread the disease even if they are pre-symptomatic or [asymptomatic](602790.md).[^cite_note-Transmission_of_COVID-19_CDNASoNG-93] Most commonly, the peak viral load in [upper respiratory tract](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Upper_respiratory_tract) samples occurs close to the time of symptom onset and declines after the first week after symptoms begin.[^cite_note-Transmission_of_COVID-19_CDNASoNG-93] Current evidence suggests a duration of viral shedding and the period of infectiousness of up to ten days following symptom onset for people with mild to moderate COVID-19, and up to 20 days for persons with severe COVID-19, including immunocompromised people.[^cite_note-Transmission_of_COVID-19_CDCClinicalQuestions-94][^cite_note-Transmission_of_COVID-19_CDNASoNG-93]

Infectious particles range in size from [aerosols](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Aerosol) that remain suspended in the air for long periods of time to larger [droplets](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Respiratory_droplet) that remain airborne briefly or fall to the ground.[^cite_note-Transmission_of_COVID-19_CDCScientificBriefTransmission-95][^cite_note-Transmission_of_COVID-19_who_transmitted-96][^cite_note-Transmission_of_COVID-19_WHO-ECDC-CDC-UK-Ca-Aus-Annals-97][^cite_note-Transmission_of_COVID-19_Tang_2021-98] Additionally, COVID-19 research has redefined the traditional understanding of how respiratory viruses are transmitted.[^cite_note-Transmission_of_COVID-19_Tang_2021-98][^cite_note-Transmission_of_COVID-19_Morawska_2021-99] The largest droplets of respiratory fluid do not travel far, but can be inhaled or land on mucous membranes on the eyes, nose, or mouth to infect.[^cite_note-Transmission_of_COVID-19_WHO-ECDC-CDC-UK-Ca-Aus-Annals-97] Aerosols are highest in concentration when people are in close proximity, which leads to easier viral transmission when people are physically close,[^cite_note-Transmission_of_COVID-19_WHO-ECDC-CDC-UK-Ca-Aus-Annals-97][^cite_note-Transmission_of_COVID-19_Tang_2021-98][^cite_note-Transmission_of_COVID-19_Morawska_2021-99] but [airborne transmission](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Airborne_transmission) can occur at longer distances, mainly in locations that are poorly ventilated;[^cite_note-Transmission_of_COVID-19_WHO-ECDC-CDC-UK-Ca-Aus-Annals-97] in those conditions small particles can remain suspended in the air for minutes to hours.[^cite_note-Transmission_of_COVID-19_WHO-ECDC-CDC-UK-Ca-Aus-Annals-97][^cite_note-100]

### Virology

Main article: [SARS-CoV-2](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/SARS-CoV-2)



Illustration of [SARSr-CoV](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/SARSr-CoV) [virion](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Virus)

Severe acute respiratory syndrome coronavirus 2 (SARS-CoV-2) is a [novel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Novel_virus) severe acute respiratory syndrome coronavirus. It was first isolated from three people with pneumonia connected to the [cluster](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Disease_cluster) of acute respiratory illness cases in Wuhan.[^cite_note-ECDC_risk_assessment-101] All structural features of the novel SARS-CoV-2 virus particle occur in related [coronaviruses](201983.md) in nature,[^cite_note-NM-20200317-102] particularly in *[Rhinolophus sinicus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rhinolophus_sinicus)* (Chinese horseshoe bats).[^cite_note-zhou20-103]

Outside the human body, the virus is destroyed by household soap which bursts its [protective bubble](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Viral_envelope).[^cite_note-NatGeoSoap-104] Hospital disinfectants, alcohols, heat, [povidone-iodine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Povidone-iodine), and [ultraviolet](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ultraviolet)-C (UV-C) irradiation are also effective disinfection methods for surfaces.[^cite_note-105]

SARS-CoV-2 is closely related to the original [SARS-CoV](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/SARS-CoV).[^cite_note-Zhu24Jan2020-106] The virus is thought to have an animal ([zoonotic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Zoonosis)) origin. Genetic analysis has revealed that the coronavirus genetically clusters with the genus *[Betacoronavirus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Betacoronavirus)*, in subgenus *[Sarbecovirus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Severe_acute_respiratory_syndrome%E2%80%93related_coronavirus)* (lineage B) together with two bat-derived strains. It is 96% identical at the whole [genome](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Genome) level to other bat coronavirus samples (BatCov [RaTG13](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/RaTG13)).[^cite_note-WHOReport24Feb2020-107][^cite_note-108][^cite_note-RathoreSingh-109] The structural proteins of SARS-CoV-2 include [membrane glycoprotein](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Coronavirus_membrane_protein) (M), [envelope protein](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Coronavirus_envelope_protein) (E), [nucleocapsid protein](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Coronavirus_nucleocapsid_protein) (N), and the [spike protein](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Coronavirus_spike_protein) (S). The M protein of SARS-CoV-2 is about 98% similar to the M protein of bat SARS-CoV, maintains around 98% homology with pangolin SARS-CoV, and has 90% homology with the M protein of SARS-CoV; whereas, the similarity is only around 38% with the M protein of [MERS-CoV](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/MERS-CoV).[^cite_note-110]

### SARS-CoV-2 variants

Main article: [Variants of SARS-CoV-2](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Variants_of_SARS-CoV-2)

The SARS-CoV-2 virus that causes COVID-19 has continuously evolved since the start of the pandemic. Once a large number of mutations have accumulated, a subtype is classified as a variant.[^cite_note-\:4-111] Major variants of concern were given a name based on letters of the [Greek alphabet](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Greek_alphabet), for example, [Alpha](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/SARS-CoV-2_Alpha_variant), [Beta](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/SARS-CoV-2_Beta_variant), [Delta](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/SARS-CoV-2_Delta_variant); after naming the [Omicron variant](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/SARS-CoV-2_Omicron_variant) in November 2021, the WHO stopped naming new variants of concern.[^cite_note-\:4-111][^cite_note-\:5-112] The [Pango system](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Phylogenetic_Assignment_of_Named_Global_Outbreak_Lineages) further specifies the [lineage](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lineage_\(evolution\)) of the variant, for instance, JN.1 for a variant active in 2024, and XFG for one in 2025.[^cite_note-\:5-112]

The Alpha variant (B.1.1.7) first appeared around autumn 2020. By the end of 2020, two other variants of concern had appeared: Gamma (P.1), which was first seen in Brazil and Beta (B.1.351), which was first detected in South Africa. Alpha came to dominate the Northern Hemisphere, Gamma in South America and Beta in Africa. The Delta variant (B.1.617.2) was first seen in India around the same time, and came to dominate globally in 2021. The Omicron variant which emerged at the end of 2021, saw a large number of mutations compared to previous variants and has been dominant since.[^cite_note-\:5-112]

New variants can quickly come to dominate global transmission when they have an advantage compared to previous variants. Within the span of about 7 weeks, the Omicron variant went from 1% of global cases to 95% by January 2022.[^cite_note-\:5-112] In addition to differences in how well the virus is transmitted, the variants differ in how well they evade vaccine protection, their severity and the risk of different [long COVID](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Long_COVID) symptoms.[^cite_note-\:5-112][^cite_note-113] The Omicron variant seems to have decreased severity compared to previous strains, but was better able to evade the original vaccines.[^cite_note-\:5-112]

## Pathophysiology



COVID‑19 [pathogenesis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pathogenesis)

The SARS-CoV-2 virus can infect a wide range of cells and systems of the body. COVID‑19 is most known for affecting the upper respiratory tract (sinuses, nose, and throat) and the lower respiratory tract (windpipe and lungs).[^cite_note-pmid33132005-114] The lungs are the organs most affected by COVID‑19 because the virus accesses host cells via the [receptor](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Receptor_\(biochemistry\)) for the enzyme [angiotensin-converting enzyme 2](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Angiotensin-converting_enzyme_2) (ACE2), which is most abundant on the surface of [type II alveolar cells](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Type_II_cell) of the lungs.[^cite_note-115] The virus uses a special surface glycoprotein called a "[spike](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Coronavirus_spike_protein)" to connect to the ACE2 receptor and enter the host cell.[^cite_note-Nature_Microbiology-116]

### Respiratory tract

Following viral entry, COVID‑19 infects the ciliated epithelium of the nasopharynx and upper airways.[^cite_note-117] Autopsies of people who died of COVID‑19 have found [diffuse alveolar damage](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Diffuse_alveolar_damage), and lymphocyte-containing inflammatory infiltrates within the lung.[^cite_note-Cureus-118]

From the [CT scans](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/CT_scan) of COVID-19 infected lungs, white patches were observed containing fluid known as [ground-glass opacity](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ground-glass_opacity) (GGO) or simply ground glass.[^cite_note-119] This tended to correlate with the clear jelly liquid found in lung autopsies of people who died of COVID-19. One possibility addressed in medical research is that [hyuralonic acid](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hyaluronic_acid) (HA) could be the leading factor for this observation of the clear jelly liquid found in the lungs, in what could be hyuralonic storm, in conjunction with [cytokine storm](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cytokine_storm).[^cite_note-120]

### Nervous system

See also: [Clouding of consciousness](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Clouding_of_consciousness)

One common symptom, loss of smell, results from [infection of the support cells of the olfactory epithelium](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Impact_of_COVID-19_on_neurological,_psychological_and_other_mental_health_outcomes), with subsequent damage to the [olfactory neurons](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Olfactory_receptor_neuron).[^cite_note-Meunier-2020-121] The involvement of both the central and peripheral nervous system in COVID‑19 has been reported in many medical publications.[^cite_note-Guerrero2020-122] It is clear that many people with COVID-19 exhibit neurological or mental health issues. The virus is not detected in the [central nervous system](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Central_nervous_system) (CNS) of the majority of people with COVID-19 who also have [neurological issues](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Impact_of_the_COVID-19_pandemic_on_neurological,_psychological_and_other_mental_health_outcomes). However, SARS-CoV-2 has been detected at low levels in the brains of those who have died from COVID‑19, but these results need to be confirmed.[^cite_note-Pezzini2020-123] While virus has been detected in [cerebrospinal fluid](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cerebrospinal_fluid) of autopsies, the exact mechanism by which it invades the CNS remains unclear and may first involve invasion of peripheral nerves given the low levels of ACE2 in the brain.[^cite_note-124][^cite_note-125][^cite_note-126] The virus may also enter the bloodstream from the lungs and cross the blood–brain barrier to gain access to the CNS, possibly within an infected [white blood cell](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/White_blood_cell).[^cite_note-Pezzini2020-123]



[Tropism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Host_tropism) and [multiple organ injuries](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Multiple_organ_dysfunction_syndrome) in SARS-CoV-2 infection

&#x20;Research conducted when Alpha was the dominant variant has suggested COVID-19 may cause brain damage.[^cite_note-127] Later research showed that all variants studied (including Omicron) killed brain cells, but the exact cells killed varied by variant.[^cite_note-128] It is unknown if such damage is temporary or permanent.[^cite_note-129][^cite_note-130] Observed individuals infected with COVID-19 (most with mild cases) experienced an additional 0.2% to 2% of brain tissue lost in regions of the brain connected to the sense of smell compared with uninfected individuals, and the overall effect on the brain was equivalent on average to at least one extra year of normal ageing; infected individuals also scored lower on several cognitive tests. All effects were more pronounced among older ages.[^cite_note-131]

### Gastrointestinal tract

The virus also affects gastrointestinal organs as ACE2 is abundantly expressed in the [glandular](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gland) cells of [gastric](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Stomach), [duodenal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Duodenum) and [rectal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rectum) [epithelium](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Epithelium)[^cite_note-Gu-2020-132] as well as [endothelial](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Endothelium) cells and [enterocytes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Enterocyte) of the [small intestine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Small_intestine).[^cite_note-pmid32343593-133]

### Cardiovascular system

The virus can cause [acute myocardial injury](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Myocardial_infarction) and chronic damage to the [cardiovascular system](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Circulatory_system).[^cite_note-134][^cite_note-Zheng-2020-135] An acute cardiac injury was found in 12% of infected people admitted to the hospital in Wuhan, China,[^cite_note-Huang24Jan2020-136] and is more frequent in severe disease.[^cite_note-137] Rates of cardiovascular symptoms are high, owing to the systemic inflammatory response and immune system disorders during disease progression, but acute myocardial injuries may also be related to ACE2 receptors in the heart.[^cite_note-Zheng-2020-135] ACE2 receptors are highly expressed in the heart and are involved in heart function.[^cite_note-Zheng-2020-135][^cite_note-138]

A high incidence of [thrombosis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Thrombosis) and [venous thromboembolism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Venous_thromboembolism) occurs in people transferred to [intensive care units](6332859.md) with COVID‑19 infections, and may be related to poor prognosis.[^cite_note-139] Blood vessel dysfunction and clot formation (as suggested by high [D-dimer](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/D-dimer) levels caused by blood clots) may contribute substantially to mortality, incidents of clots leading to [pulmonary embolisms](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pulmonary_embolism), and [ischaemic events](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Stroke) (strokes) within the brain found as complications leading to death in people infected with COVID‑19.[^cite_note-Science-140] Infection may initiate a chain of [vasoconstrictive responses](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vasoconstriction) within the body, including pulmonary vasoconstriction – a possible mechanism in which oxygenation decreases during pneumonia.[^cite_note-Science-140] Furthermore, damage of [arterioles](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arterioles) and [capillaries](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Capillaries) was found in brain tissue samples of people who died from COVID‑19.[^cite_note-141][^cite_note-142]

COVID‑19 may also cause substantial structural changes to [blood cells](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Blood_cell), sometimes persisting for months after hospital discharge.[^cite_note-143] [A low level of blood lymphocytess](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lymphopenia) may result from the virus acting through ACE2-related entry into lymphocytes.[^cite_note-144]

### Kidneys

Another common cause of death is complications related to the [kidneys](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kidney).[^cite_note-Science-140] Early reports show that up to 30% of people hospitalised with COVID-19 both in China and in New York have experienced some injury to their kidneys, including some persons with no previous kidney problems.[^cite_note-145]

### Immunopathology



Key components of the [adaptive immune response](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Adaptive_immune_system) to SARS-CoV-2

Although SARS-CoV-2 has a tropism for ACE2-expressing epithelial cells of the respiratory tract, people with severe COVID‑19 have symptoms of systemic hyperinflammation. Clinical laboratory findings of elevated [IL‑2](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Interleukin_2), [IL‑6](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Interleukin_6), [IL‑7](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Interleukin_7), as well as the following suggest an underlying immunopathology:[^cite_note-Huang24Jan2020-136]

- [Granulocyte-macrophage colony-stimulating factor](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Granulocyte-macrophage_colony-stimulating_factor) (GM‑CSF)

- [Interferon gamma-induced protein 10](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/CXCL10) (IP‑10)

- [Monocyte chemoattractant protein 1](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Monocyte_chemoattractant_protein-1) (MCP1)

- [Macrophage inflammatory protein 1‑alpha](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/CCL3) (MIP‑1‑alpha)

- [Tumour necrosis factor](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tumour_necrosis_factor) (TNF‑α) indicative of [cytokine release syndrome](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cytokine_release_syndrome) (CRS)

[Interferon alpha](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Interferon-alpha) plays a complex, Janus-faced role in the pathogenesis of COVID-19. Although it promotes the elimination of virus-infected cells, it also upregulates the expression of ACE-2, thereby helping the SARS-Cov2 virus enter cells and to replicate.[^cite_note-146][^cite_note-147] A competition of negative feedback loops (via protective effects of interferon alpha) and positive feedback loops (via upregulation of ACE-2) is assumed to determine the fate of people with COVID-19.[^cite_note-148]

Additionally, people with COVID‑19 and [acute respiratory distress syndrome](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Acute_respiratory_distress_syndrome) (ARDS) have classical [serum](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Serum_\(blood\)) [biomarkers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Biomarker_\(medicine\)) of CRS, including elevated [C-reactive protein](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/C-reactive_protein) (CRP), [lactate dehydrogenase](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lactate_dehydrogenase) (LDH), [D-dimer](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/D-dimer), and [ferritin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ferritin).[^cite_note-149]

Systemic inflammation results in [vasodilation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vasodilation), allowing inflammatory lymphocytic and monocytic infiltration of the lung and the heart. In particular, pathogenic GM-CSF-secreting [T cells](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/T_cell) were shown to correlate with the recruitment of inflammatory IL-6-secreting [monocytes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Monocyte) and severe lung pathology in people with COVID‑19.[^cite_note-150] Lymphocytic infiltrates have also been reported at autopsy.[^cite_note-Cureus-118]

### Viral and host factors

#### Virus proteins



The association between SARS-CoV-2 and the [Renin-Angiotensin-Aldosterone System](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Renin%E2%80%93angiotensin_system)

Multiple viral and host factors affect the pathogenesis of the virus. The [S-protein](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/S_protein), otherwise known as the spike protein, is the viral component that attaches to the host receptor via the [ACE2](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Angiotensin-converting_enzyme_2) receptors. It includes two subunits: S1 and S2.[^cite_note-151]

- S1 determines the virus-host range and cellular tropism via the receptor-binding domain.

- S2 mediates the membrane fusion of the virus to its potential cell host via the H1 and HR2, which are [heptad repeat](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Heptad_repeat) regions.

Studies have shown that S1 domain induced [IgG](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/IgG) and [IgA](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/IgA) antibody levels at a much higher capacity. It is the focus spike proteins expression that are involved in many effective COVID‑19 vaccines.[^cite_note-pmid33340022-152]

The M protein is the viral protein responsible for the transmembrane transport of nutrients. It is the cause of the bud release and the formation of the viral envelope.[^cite_note-Boopathi-2020-153] The N and E protein are accessory proteins that interfere with the host's immune response.[^cite_note-Boopathi-2020-153]

#### Host factors

Human [angiotensin converting enzyme 2](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Angiotensin_converting_enzyme_2) (hACE2) is the host factor that SARS-CoV-2 virus targets causing COVID‑19. Theoretically, the usage of [angiotensin receptor blockers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Angiotensin_II_receptor_blocker) (ARB) and [ACE inhibitors](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/ACE_inhibitor) upregulating ACE2 expression might increase [morbidity](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Morbidity) with COVID‑19, though animal data suggest some potential protective effect of ARB; however no clinical studies have proven susceptibility or outcomes. Until further data is available, guidelines and recommendations for people with hypertension remain.[^cite_note-154]

The effect of the virus on ACE2 cell surfaces leads to leukocytic infiltration, increased blood vessel permeability, alveolar wall permeability, as well as decreased secretion of lung surfactants. These effects cause the majority of the respiratory symptoms. However, the aggravation of local inflammation causes a cytokine storm eventually leading to a [systemic inflammatory response syndrome](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Systemic_inflammatory_response_syndrome).[^cite_note-155]

Among healthy adults not exposed to SARS-CoV-2, about 35% have [CD4<sup>+</sup> T cells](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/CD4+_T_cell) that recognise the SARS-CoV-2 [S protein](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Peplomer) (particularly the S2 subunit) and about 50% react to other proteins of the virus, suggesting [cross-reactivity](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cross-reactivity) from previous [common colds](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Common_cold) caused by other coronaviruses.[^cite_note-156]

It is unknown whether different persons use similar antibody genes in response to COVID‑19.[^cite_note-157]

### Host cytokine response



Mild versus severe [immune response](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Immune_system) during [virus infection](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Viral_disease)

The severity of the inflammation can be attributed to the severity of what is known as the [cytokine storm](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cytokine_storm).[^cite_note-pmid32474885-158] Levels of [interleukin 1B](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Interleukin_1_beta), [interferon-gamma](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Interferon_gamma), interferon-inducible protein 10, and monocyte chemoattractant protein 1 were all associated with COVID‑19 disease severity. Treatment has been proposed to combat the cytokine storm as it remains to be one of the leading causes of [morbidity](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Morbidity) and mortality in COVID‑19 disease.[^cite_note-159]

A cytokine storm is due to an acute hyperinflammatory response that is responsible for clinical illness in an array of diseases but in COVID‑19, it is related to worse prognosis and increased fatality. The storm causes acute respiratory distress syndrome, blood clotting events such as strokes, [myocardial infarction](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Myocardial_infarction), [encephalitis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Encephalitis), [acute kidney injury](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Acute_kidney_injury), and [vasculitis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vasculitis). The production of [IL-1](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Interleukin_1), [IL-2](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Interleukin_2), [IL-6](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Interleukin_6), [TNF-alpha](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tumor_necrosis_factor), and [interferon-gamma](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Interferon_gamma), all essential components of normal immune responses, inadvertently become the causes of a cytokine storm. The cells of the [central nervous system](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Central_nervous_system), the [microglia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Microglia), [neurons](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Neuron), and [astrocytes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Astrocyte), are also involved in the release of [pro-inflammatory cytokines](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pro-inflammatory_cytokine) affecting the nervous system, and effects of cytokine storms toward the CNS are not uncommon.[^cite_note-160] Interestingly patients with obesity, who tend to develop more severe disease[^cite_note-161], demonstrate reduced inflammatory responses in their lungs[^cite_note-162]. This was most marked in the [type I interferon responses](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Interferon_type_I) and TNF-alpha, although the TNF response was not attenuated in the blood. The supposition is that the reduced pulmonary anti-viral defences leads to increase viral proliferation and greater damage, leading to more severe respiratory failure.&#x20;

### Pregnancy response

Early in the COVID-19 pandemic, due to pregnant individuals being prone to severe complications from being infected with other types of coronaviruses, they were identified as a vulnerable group and advised to take supplementary preventive measures.[^cite_note-Wastnedge_2021-163]

Physiological responses to pregnancy can include:

- Immunological: The immunological response to COVID-19, like other viruses, depends on a working immune system. It adapts during pregnancy to allow the development of the foetus whose genetic load is only partially shared with their mother, leading to a different immunological reaction to infections during pregnancy.

  [^cite_note-Wastnedge_2021-163]

- Respiratory: Many factors can make pregnant women more vulnerable to hard respiratory infections. One of them is the total reduction of the lungs' capacity and inability to clear secretions.

  [^cite_note-Wastnedge_2021-163]

- Coagulation: During pregnancy, there are higher levels of circulating coagulation factors, and the pathogenesis of SARS-CoV-2 infection can be implicated. The thromboembolic events with associated mortality are a risk for pregnant women.

  [^cite_note-Wastnedge_2021-163]

A multinational cohort study conducted between March and October 2020 found that being infected with COVID-19 led to increased rates of [maternal mortality](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Maternal_mortality), as well as heightening the risks of [pre-eclampsia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pre-eclampsia), [preterm birth](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Preterm_birth), illness in infants, and illness or miscarriage in [perinatal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Perinatal) foetuses.[^cite_note-villar-et-al-2021-164][^cite_note-Wastnedge_2021-163] At the same time, a retrospective cohort study using data recorded in the United States between February and May 2020 found that there was no correlation between COVID-19 infection during first trimester pregnancy and the risks of miscarrying.[^cite_note-cosma-et-al-2021-165]

Unvaccinated women in later stages of pregnancy with COVID-19 are more likely than other people to need intensive care. Babies born to mothers with COVID-19 are more likely to have breathing problems. Pregnant women are strongly encouraged to get [vaccinated](63319438.md).[^cite_note-166]

## Diagnosis

COVID‑19 can provisionally be diagnosed on the basis of symptoms and confirmed using [reverse transcription polymerase chain reaction](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Reverse_transcription_polymerase_chain_reaction) (RT-PCR) or other [nucleic acid testing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nucleic_acid_test) of infected secretions.[^cite_note-pmid32621814-167][^cite_note-jTxKm-168] Along with laboratory testing, chest CT scans may be helpful to diagnose COVID‑19 in individuals with a high clinical suspicion of infection.[^cite_note-Salehi-2020-169] Detection of a past infection is possible with [serological tests](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Serology), which detect [antibodies](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Antibody) produced by the body in response to the infection.[^cite_note-pmid32621814-167]

### Viral testing

Main article: [COVID-19 testing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/COVID-19_testing)



Demonstration of a [nasopharyngeal swab](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nasopharyngeal_swab) for [COVID‑19 testing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/COVID%E2%80%9119_testing)

The standard methods of testing for presence of SARS-CoV-2 are [nucleic acid tests](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nucleic_acid_test),[^cite_note-pmid32621814-167][^cite_note-20200130cdc-170] which detects the presence of viral RNA fragments.[^cite_note-WHO_InterimGuidance-171] As these tests detect RNA but not infectious virus, its "ability to determine duration of infectivity of patients is limited".[^cite_note-2k0iS-172] The test is typically done on respiratory samples obtained by a [nasopharyngeal swab](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nasopharyngeal_swab); however, a nasal swab or sputum sample may also be used.[^cite_note-CDC2020Testing-173][^cite_note-20200129cdc-174] Results are generally available within hours.[^cite_note-pmid32621814-167]

Several laboratories and companies have developed serological tests, which detect antibodies produced by the body in response to infection. Some have been evaluated by [Public Health England](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Public_Health_England) and approved for use in the UK.[^cite_note-independent9515466-175]

The [University of Oxford](31797.md)'s CEBM has pointed to mounting evidence[^cite_note-yFMEP-176][^cite_note-2exX5-177] that "a good proportion of 'new' mild cases and people re-testing positives after quarantine or discharge from hospital are not infectious, but are simply clearing harmless virus particles which their immune system has efficiently dealt with".[^cite_note-PQGAv-178]

### Imaging



A [CT](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Computed_tomography) scan of a person with COVID-19 shows lesions (bright regions) in the lungs



CT scan of rapid progression stage of COVID-19



Chest X-ray showing COVID‑19 pneumonia

Chest CT scans may be helpful to diagnose COVID‑19 in individuals with a high clinical suspicion of infection but are not recommended for routine screening.[^cite_note-Salehi-2020-169][^cite_note-acr.org-179] Bilateral multilobar [ground-glass opacities](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ground-glass_opacity) with a peripheral, asymmetric, and posterior distribution are common in early infection.[^cite_note-Salehi-2020-169][^cite_note-180] Subpleural dominance, [crazy paving](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Crazy_paving_\(medicine\)) (lobular septal thickening with variable alveolar filling), and [consolidation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pulmonary_consolidation) may appear as the disease progresses.[^cite_note-Salehi-2020-169][^cite_note-181] Characteristic imaging features on chest [radiographs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Radiograph) and [computed tomography](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Computed_tomography) (CT) of people who are symptomatic include asymmetric peripheral ground-glass opacities without [pleural effusions](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pleural_effusion).[^cite_note-AJR-182]

Many groups have created [COVID‑19 datasets](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/COVID%E2%80%9119_datasets) that include imagery such as the [Italian Radiological Society](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Italian_Society_of_Medical_and_Interventional_Radiology) which has compiled an international online database of imaging findings for confirmed cases.[^cite_note-Cx70C-183] Due to overlap with other infections such as [adenovirus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Adenovirus), imaging without confirmation by rRT-PCR is of limited [specificity](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Specificity_\(statistics\)) in identifying COVID‑19.[^cite_note-AJR-182] A large study in China compared chest CT results to PCR and demonstrated that though imaging is less specific for the infection, it is faster and more [sensitive](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sensitivity_\(statistics\)).[^cite_note-jTxKm-168]

### Coding

In late 2019, the WHO assigned emergency [ICD-10](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/ICD-10) disease codes U07.1 for deaths from lab-confirmed SARS-CoV-2 infection and U07.2 for deaths from clinically or epidemiologically diagnosed COVID‑19 without lab-confirmed SARS-CoV-2 infection.[^cite_note-ICD10_2019_U07p2-184]

### Pathology

The main pathological findings at autopsy are:

- [Macroscopy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Macroscopic_scale): [pericarditis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pericarditis), [lung consolidation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lung_consolidation) and [pulmonary oedema](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pulmonary_edema)

  [^cite_note-Cureus-118]

- Lung findings:&#x20;

  - Minor serous [exudation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Exudate), minor [fibrin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fibrin) exudation

    [^cite_note-Cureus-118]

  - Pulmonary oedema, [pneumocyte](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pneumocyte) [hyperplasia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hyperplasia), large atypical [pneumocytes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pneumocyte), interstitial [inflammation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Inflammation) with [lymphocytic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lymphocytic) [infiltration](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Infiltration_\(medical\)) and [multinucleated giant cell](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Giant_cell) formation

    [^cite_note-Cureus-118]

  - [Diffuse alveolar damage](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Diffuse_alveolar_damage) (DAD) with diffuse [alveolar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pulmonary_alveolus) [exudates](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Exudates). DAD is the cause of [acute respiratory distress syndrome](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Acute_respiratory_distress_syndrome) (ARDS) and severe [hypoxaemia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hypoxemia).

    [^cite_note-Cureus-118]

  - [Organisation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Healing) of [exudates](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Exudate) in [alveolar cavities](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pulmonary_alveolus) and [pulmonary interstitial fibrosis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pulmonary_fibrosis)

    [^cite_note-Cureus-118]

  - [Plasmocytosis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Plasma_cell) in [bronchoalveolar lavage](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bronchoalveolar_lavage) (BAL)

    [^cite_note-185]

- Blood and vessels: [disseminated intravascular coagulation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Disseminated_intravascular_coagulation) (DIC);

  [^cite_note-186]

  &#x20;[leukoerythroblastic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Leukoerythroblastic) reaction,

  [^cite_note-187]

  &#x20;[endotheliitis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Endotheliitis),

  [^cite_note-SatturwarFowkes2021-188]

  &#x20;[hemophagocytosis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hemophagocytosis)

  [^cite_note-SatturwarFowkes2021-188]

- Heart: [cardiac muscle cell](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cardiac_muscle_cell) necrosis

  [^cite_note-SatturwarFowkes2021-188]

- Liver: microvesicular [steatosis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Steatosis)

  [^cite_note-Cureus-118]

- Nose: [shedding of olfactory epithelium](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Impact_of_COVID-19_on_neurological,_psychological_and_other_mental_health_outcomes)

  [^cite_note-Meunier-2020-121]

- Brain: [infarction](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cerebral_infarction)

  [^cite_note-SatturwarFowkes2021-188]

- Kidneys: acute tubular damage.

  [^cite_note-SatturwarFowkes2021-188]

- Spleen: [white pulp](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/White_pulp) depletion.

  [^cite_note-SatturwarFowkes2021-188]

## Prevention

Further information: [COVID-19 vaccine](63319438.md), [Workplace hazard controls for COVID-19](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Workplace_hazard_controls_for_COVID-19), [Pandemic prevention](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pandemic_prevention), [Non-pharmaceutical intervention](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Non-pharmaceutical_intervention_\(epidemiology\)), [Preparations prior to COVID-19](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pandemic_predictions_and_preparations_prior_to_the_COVID-19_pandemic), [COVID-19 surveillance](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/COVID-19_surveillance), and [COVID-19 apps](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/COVID-19_apps)



Without pandemic containment measures – such as social distancing, vaccination, and face masks – pathogens can spread exponentially.

[^cite_note-Science_20200515-189]

&#x20;This graphic shows how early adoption of containment measures tends to protect wider swaths of the population.

Preventive measures to reduce the chances of infection include getting vaccinated, staying at home, wearing a mask in public, avoiding crowded places, keeping distance from others, ventilating indoor spaces, managing potential exposure durations,[^cite_note-190] washing hands with soap and water often and for at least 20 seconds, practising good respiratory hygiene, and avoiding touching the eyes, nose, or mouth with unwashed hands.[^cite_note-CDC042020-191][^cite_note-192]

Those diagnosed with COVID‑19 or who believe they may be infected are advised by the CDC to stay home except to get medical care, call ahead before visiting a healthcare provider, [wear a face mask](#Face_masks_and_respiratory_hygiene) before entering the healthcare provider's office and when in any room or vehicle with another person, cover coughs and sneezes with a tissue, regularly wash hands with soap and water and avoid sharing personal household items.[^cite_note-CDC2020IfSick-193][^cite_note-194]

The first [COVID‑19 vaccine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/COVID%E2%80%9119_vaccine) was granted regulatory approval on 2 December 2020 by the UK medicines regulator [MHRA](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Medicines_and_Healthcare_products_Regulatory_Agency).[^cite_note-ukgov12-2-195] It was evaluated for [emergency use authorisation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Emergency_use_authorisation) (EUA) status by the US [FDA](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Food_and_Drugs_Administration), and in several other countries.[^cite_note-mueller-196] Initially, the US [National Institutes of Health](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Institutes_of_Health) guidelines do not recommend any medication for prevention of COVID‑19, before or after exposure to the SARS-CoV-2 virus, outside the setting of a clinical trial.[^cite_note-NIHGuidelines2020-197][^cite_note-Sanders202022-76] Without a vaccine, other prophylactic measures, or effective treatments, a key part of managing COVID‑19 is trying to decrease and delay the epidemic peak, known as "flattening the [curve](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Epidemic_curve)".[^cite_note-Lancet2020Flatten-198] This is done by slowing the infection rate to decrease the risk of health services being overwhelmed, allowing for better treatment of active cases, and delaying additional cases until effective treatments or a vaccine become available.[^cite_note-Lancet2020Flatten-198][^cite_note-Wiles-199]

### Vaccine

Main article: [COVID-19 vaccine](63319438.md)



Different vaccine candidate types in development for SARS-CoV-2



Death rates for unvaccinated Americans substantially exceeded those who were vaccinated, with bivalent boosters further reducing the death rate.

[^cite_note-COVIDmortality_2021_2023-200]

Major vaccines include the [Pfizer–BioNTech mRNA vaccine](65797150.md), [Moderna mRNA vaccine](63399660.md), and the [Novavax protein subunit vaccine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Novavax_COVID-19_vaccine).[^cite_note-COVID-19_vaccine_VaccineKnowledgeCOVID-201] With the emergence of new [SARS-CoV-2 variants](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Variants_of_SARS-CoV-2), the original vaccines—particularly [Pfizer](62304.md)–[BioNTech](64671486.md) and [Moderna](39743243.md) vaccines—have been updated. These "variant-adapted" vaccines are offered as [booster doses](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Booster_dose).[^cite_note-COVID-19_vaccine_VaccineKnowledgeCOVID-201] The immunity from the vaccines also wanes over time, requiring people to get boosters to maintain protection.[^cite_note-COVID-19_vaccine_BritannicaCOVID-202]

Common side effects of COVID‑19 vaccines include soreness, fatigue, headache, [myalgia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Myalgia) (muscle pain), and [arthralgia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arthralgia) (joint pain), which resolve without medical treatment within a few days.[^cite_note-COVID-19_vaccine_COVID-19_vaccine_WHO-203] COVID‑19 vaccination is safe for people who are pregnant or are breastfeeding.[^cite_note-COVID-19_vaccine_breastf-204]

The COVID‑19 vaccines are widely credited for their role in reducing the spread of COVID‑19 and reducing the severity and death caused by COVID‑19.[^cite_note-COVID-19_vaccine_BritannicaCOVID-202][^cite_note-205] The Australian-based [medical journal](29500858.md) *[Journal of Paediatrics and Child Health](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Journal_of_Paediatrics_and_Child_Health)* estimated that between 14.4 and 19.8 million deaths were prevented by the vaccine.[^cite_note-206] Many countries implemented phased distribution plans that prioritized those at highest risk of complications, such as the elderly, and those at high risk of exposure, such as [healthcare workers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Health_professional).[^cite_note-COVID-19_vaccine_Beaumont-207][^cite_note-COVID-19_vaccine_Wang1-208] By December 2020, more than 10 billion vaccine doses had been preordered,[^cite_note-COVID-19_vaccine_mullard-209] with about half of the doses purchased by [high-income countries](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/World_Bank_high-income_economy) comprising 14% of the world's population.[^cite_note-COVID-19_vaccine_So-210] As of August 2024, over 13 billion doses of COVID‑19 vaccines have been administered worldwide.[^cite_note-COVID-19_vaccine_Richie_2021-211]

### Face masks and respiratory hygiene

Main article: [Face masks during the COVID-19 pandemic](63631542.md)



Masks with an exhalation valve. The valves are a weak point that can transmit the viruses outwards.



US Ambassador to Indonesia [Sung Kim](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sung_Kim) accompanied by local officials at the [Presidential Palace](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Merdeka_Palace) wearing face masks amid the COVID-19 pandemic

In community and healthcare settings, the use of face masks was intended as [source control](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Source_control_\(respiratory_disease\)) to limit [transmission](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Transmission_of_COVID-19) of the virus and for personal protection to prevent infection.[^cite_note-Face_masks_during_the_COVID-19_pandemic_AR2021-212] When properly worn, face masks can limit the [respiratory droplets](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Respiratory_droplet) and [aerosols](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Airborne_transmission) that are spread by infected individuals, helping protect healthy individuals from infection.[^cite_note-Face_masks_during_the_COVID-19_pandemic_Matuschek-213][^cite_note-Face_masks_during_the_COVID-19_pandemic_interplay-214]

Scientific studies concluded that the use of face masks was effective in protecting the individual using them against COVID-19.[^cite_note-Face_masks_during_the_COVID-19_pandemic_Matuschek-213][^cite_note-Face_masks_during_the_COVID-19_pandemic_Talic-215][^cite_note-Face_masks_during_the_COVID-19_pandemic_cdc-sci-216] Various [case-control](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Case%E2%80%93control_study) and [population-based](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Population_based_study) studies also showed that the increased use of masks in a community reduced the spread of SARS-CoV-2,[^cite_note-Face_masks_during_the_COVID-19_pandemic_Talic-215][^cite_note-Face_masks_during_the_COVID-19_pandemic_cdc-sci-216] although there was a paucity of evidence from [randomized controlled trials](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Randomized_controlled_trial) (RCTs).[^cite_note-Face_masks_during_the_COVID-19_pandemic_cochrane-217][^cite_note-Face_masks_during_the_COVID-19_pandemic_royalsoc-218] Masks varied in their effectiveness. Fitted [N95s](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/N95_respirator) outperform surgical masks,[^cite_note-Face_masks_during_the_COVID-19_pandemic_Ju-219][^cite_note-Face_masks_during_the_COVID-19_pandemic_norespirator-220] while cloth masks provided marginal protection.[^cite_note-Face_masks_during_the_COVID-19_pandemic_Ataei-221][^cite_note-Face_masks_during_the_COVID-19_pandemic_cloth_v_surgical-222] During the public health emergency, governments widely recommended (and, in some jurisdictions, mandated) the wearing of masks. Prominent national and intergovernmental health agencies and their leaders recommended the use of masks to reduce transmission, including the [WHO](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/WHO), [American](6811.md), [European](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/ECDC), and [Chinese](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chinese_Center_for_Disease_Control_and_Prevention) Centers for [Disease Control](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Disease_Control) and Prevention.

### Indoor ventilation and avoiding crowded indoor spaces

The CDC states that avoiding crowded indoor spaces reduces the risk of COVID-19 infection.[^cite_note-CDC-2020b-223] When indoors, increasing the rate of air change, decreasing recirculation of air and increasing the use of outdoor air can reduce transmission.[^cite_note-CDC-2020b-223][^cite_note-ecdc_transmission2-224] The WHO recommends [ventilation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ventilation_\(architecture\)) and [air filtration](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Air_filtration) in public spaces to help clear out infectious aerosols.[^cite_note-CDCasof07092020-225][^cite_note-KoFZO-226][^cite_note-Lancetdroplet05272020-227]

Exhaled respiratory particles can build-up within enclosed spaces with inadequate [ventilation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ventilation_\(architecture\)). The risk of COVID‑19 infection increases especially in spaces where people engage in physical exertion or raise their voice (e.g., exercising, shouting, singing) as this increases exhalation of respiratory droplets. Prolonged exposure to these conditions, typically more than 15 minutes, leads to higher risk of infection.[^cite_note-CDC-2020b-223]

[Displacement ventilation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Displacement_ventilation) with large natural inlets can move stale air directly to the exhaust in [laminar flow](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Laminar_flow) while significantly reducing the concentration of droplets and particles. [Passive ventilation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Passive_ventilation) reduces energy consumption and maintenance costs but may lack [controllability](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Demand_controlled_ventilation) and [heat recovery](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Heat_recovery_ventilation). Displacement ventilation can also be achieved mechanically with higher energy and maintenance costs. The use of large ducts and openings helps to prevent mixing in closed environments. Recirculation and mixing should be avoided because recirculation prevents dilution of harmful particles and redistributes possibly contaminated air, and mixing increases the concentration and range of infectious particles and keeps larger particles in the air.[^cite_note-228]

### Hand-washing and hygiene

Main article: [Hand washing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hand_washing)



Students in [Rwanda](25645.md) hand washing and wearing face masks during the [COVID‑19 pandemic in the country](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/COVID-19_pandemic_in_Rwanda)

Thorough hand hygiene after any cough or sneeze is required.[^cite_note-nhs.uk-2020-229] The WHO also recommends that individuals wash hands often with soap and water for at least 20 seconds, especially after going to the toilet or when hands are visibly dirty, before eating and after blowing one's nose.[^cite_note-WHO-2020b-230] When soap and water are not available, the CDC recommends using an alcohol-based [hand sanitiser](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hand_sanitiser) with at least 60% alcohol.[^cite_note-231] For areas where commercial hand sanitisers are not readily available, the WHO provides two formulations for local production. In these formulations, the antimicrobial activity arises from [ethanol](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ethanol) or [isopropanol](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Isopropanol). [Hydrogen peroxide](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hydrogen_peroxide) is used to help eliminate [bacterial spores](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bacterial_spore) in the alcohol; it is "not an active substance for hand [antisepsis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Antisepsis)". [Glycerol](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Glycerol) is added as a [humectant](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Humectant).[^cite_note-232]

### Social distancing

Main article: [Social distancing measures related to the COVID-19 pandemic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Social_distancing_measures_related_to_the_COVID-19_pandemic)

Social distancing (also known as physical distancing) includes [infection control](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Infection_control) actions intended to slow the spread of the disease by minimising close contact between individuals. Methods include quarantines; travel restrictions; and the closing of schools, workplaces, stadiums, theatres, or shopping centres. Individuals may apply social distancing methods by staying at home, limiting travel, avoiding crowded areas, using no-contact greetings, and physically distancing themselves from others.[^cite_note-233]

In 2020, outbreaks occurred in prisons due to crowding and an inability to enforce adequate social distancing.[^cite_note-Hawks2020-234][^cite_note-235] In the United States, the prisoner population is ageing and many of them are at high risk for poor outcomes from COVID‑19 due to high rates of coexisting heart and lung disease, and poor access to high-quality healthcare.[^cite_note-Hawks2020-234]

### Surface cleaning

After being expelled from the body, coronaviruses can survive on surfaces for hours to days. If a person touches the dirty surface, they may deposit the virus at the eyes, nose, or mouth where it can enter the body and cause infection.[^cite_note-CDCTrans-236] Evidence indicates that contact with infected surfaces is not the main driver of COVID‑19,[^cite_note-237][^cite_note-238][^cite_note-cdc.gov-239] leading to recommendations for optimised disinfection procedures to avoid issues such as the increase of [antimicrobial resistance](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Antimicrobial_resistance) through the use of inappropriate cleaning products and processes.[^cite_note-disinfection-foodindus-240][^cite_note-241] [Deep cleaning](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Deep_clean_\(COVID-19\)) and other surface sanitation has been criticised as [hygiene theatre](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hygiene_theater), giving a false sense of security against something primarily spread through the air.[^cite_note-242][^cite_note-243]

The amount of time that the virus can survive depends significantly on the type of surface, the temperature, and the humidity.[^cite_note-Bueckert-2020-244] Coronaviruses die quickly when exposed to the [UV light](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/UV_light) in [sunlight](27979.md).[^cite_note-Bueckert-2020-244] Like other enveloped viruses, SARS-CoV-2 survives longest when the temperature is at [room temperature](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Room_temperature) or lower, and when the [relative humidity](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Relative_humidity) is low (<50%).[^cite_note-Bueckert-2020-244]

On many surfaces, including glass, some types of plastic, stainless steel, and skin, the virus can remain infective for several days indoors at room temperature, or even about a week under ideal conditions.[^cite_note-Bueckert-2020-244][^cite_note-245] On some surfaces, including cotton fabric and copper, the virus usually dies after a few hours.[^cite_note-Bueckert-2020-244] The virus dies faster on porous surfaces than on non-porous surfaces due to capillary action within pores and faster aerosol droplet evaporation.[^cite_note-246][^cite_note-cdc.gov-239][^cite_note-Bueckert-2020-244] However, of the many surfaces tested, two with the longest survival times are N95 respirator masks and surgical masks, both of which are considered porous surfaces.[^cite_note-Bueckert-2020-244]

The CDC says that in most situations, cleaning surfaces with soap or detergent, not disinfecting, is enough to reduce risk of transmission.[^cite_note-cdc.gov-239][^cite_note-247] The CDC recommends that if a COVID‑19 case is suspected or confirmed at a facility such as an office or day care, all areas such as offices, bathrooms, common areas, shared electronic equipment like tablets, touch screens, keyboards, remote controls, and ATMs used by the ill persons should be disinfected.[^cite_note-sxygw-248] Surfaces may be decontaminated with the following:

- 62–71% [ethanol](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ethanol)

- 50–100% [isopropanol](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Isopropanol)

- 0.1% [sodium hypochlorite](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sodium_hypochlorite)

- 0.5% [hydrogen peroxide](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hydrogen_peroxide)

- 0.2–7.5% [povidone-iodine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Povidone-iodine)

- 50–200 ppm [hypochlorous acid](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hypochlorous_acid)

Other solutions, such as [benzalkonium chloride](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Benzalkonium_chloride) and [chlorhexidine gluconate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chlorhexidine_gluconate), are less effective. [Ultraviolet germicidal irradiation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ultraviolet_germicidal_irradiation) may also be used,[^cite_note-CDCasof07092020-225] although popular devices require 5–10 min exposure and may deteriorate some materials over time.[^cite_note-249]

### Self-isolation

[Self-isolation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Self-isolation) at home has been recommended for those diagnosed with COVID‑19 and those who suspect they have been infected. Health agencies have issued detailed instructions for proper self-isolation.[^cite_note-pmid33012884-250] Many governments have mandated or recommended self-quarantine for entire populations. The strongest self-quarantine instructions have been issued to those in high-risk groups.[^cite_note-lZR3i-251] Those who may have been exposed to someone with COVID‑19 and those who have recently travelled to a country or region with the widespread transmission have been advised to self-quarantine for 14 days from the time of last possible exposure.[^cite_note-252]

### International travel-related control measures

A 2021 Cochrane rapid review found that based upon low-certainty evidence, international travel-related control measures such as restricting cross-border travel may help to contain the spread of COVID‑19.[^cite_note-Burns-2021-253] Additionally, symptom/exposure-based screening measures at borders may miss many positive cases.[^cite_note-Burns-2021-253] While test-based border screening measures may be more effective, it could also miss many positive cases if only conducted upon arrival without follow-up. The review concluded that a minimum 10-day quarantine may be beneficial in preventing the spread of COVID‑19 and may be more effective if combined with an additional control measure like border screening.[^cite_note-Burns-2021-253]

## Treatment

Main article: [Treatment and management of COVID-19](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Treatment_and_management_of_COVID-19)

&#x20;The [treatment and management of COVID-19](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Treatment_and_management_of_COVID-19) combines both [supportive care](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Supportive_care), which includes treatment to [relieve symptoms](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Symptomatic_treatment), [fluid therapy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fluid_replacement), [oxygen support](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Oxygen_support) as needed,[^cite_note-Treatment_and_management_of_COVID-19_NatureDale_Fisher_&_David_Heymann-254][^cite_note-Treatment_and_management_of_COVID-19_KuiFang2020-255][^cite_note-Treatment_and_management_of_COVID-19_Wang_Du_Zhu_Cao_2020_p.-256] and a growing list of approved medications. Highly effective vaccines have reduced mortality related to SARS-CoV-2; for those awaiting vaccination, as well as for the estimated millions of immunocompromised persons who are unlikely to respond robustly to vaccination, treatment remains important.[^cite_note-257] Some people may experience persistent symptoms or disability after recovery from the infection, known as [long COVID](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Long_COVID), but there is still limited information on the best management and rehabilitation for this condition.[^cite_note-Treatment_and_management_of_COVID-19_NIHGuidelines2020-258]

Most cases of COVID-19 are mild. In these, supportive care includes medication such as [paracetamol](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paracetamol) or [NSAIDs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/NSAID) to relieve symptoms (fever, body aches, cough), proper intake of fluids, rest, and [nasal breathing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Obligate_nasal_breathing).[^cite_note-259][^cite_note-Treatment_and_management_of_COVID-19_AutoDW-63-260][^cite_note-261][^cite_note-262] Good personal hygiene and a [healthy diet](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Healthy_diet) are also recommended.[^cite_note-263] As of April 2020 the U.S. [Centers for Disease Control and Prevention](6811.md) (CDC) recommended that those who suspect they are carrying the virus isolate themselves at home and wear a face mask.[^cite_note-Treatment_and_management_of_COVID-19_CDC2020IfSick-264] As of November 2020 use of the [glucocorticoid](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Glucocorticoid) [dexamethasone](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dexamethasone) had been strongly recommended in those severe cases treated in hospital with low oxygen levels, to reduce the risk of death.[^cite_note-Treatment_and_management_of_COVID-19_\:3-265][^cite_note-Treatment_and_management_of_COVID-19_\:0-266][^cite_note-Treatment_and_management_of_COVID-19_\:1-267] [Noninvasive ventilation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Noninvasive_ventilation) and, ultimately, admission to an [intensive care unit](6332859.md) for [mechanical ventilation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mechanical_ventilation) may be required to support breathing.[^cite_note-Treatment_and_management_of_COVID-19_NIHGuidelines2020-258] [Extracorporeal membrane oxygenation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Extracorporeal_membrane_oxygenation) (ECMO) has been used to address respiratory failure, but its benefits are still under consideration.[^cite_note-Treatment_and_management_of_COVID-19_Guan_Ni_Hu_Liang_p.-268][^cite_note-Treatment_and_management_of_COVID-19_Henry_2020_p.-269] Some of the cases of severe disease course are caused by systemic hyper-inflammation, the so-called *[cytokine storm](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cytokine_storm)*.[^cite_note-270]

Although several medications have been approved in different countries as of April 2022, not all countries have these medications. Patients with mild to moderate symptoms who are in the risk groups can take [nirmatrelvir/ritonavir](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nirmatrelvir/ritonavir) (marketed as Paxlovid) or [remdesivir](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Remdesivir), either of which reduces the risk of serious illness or hospitalization.[^cite_note-271][^cite_note-272] In the US, the [Biden Administration COVID-19 action plan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Biden_Administration_COVID-19_action_plan) includes the Test to Treat initiative, where people can go to a pharmacy, take a COVID test, and immediately receive free Paxlovid if they test positive.[^cite_note-273] In November 2021, the UK approved the use of [molnupiravir](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Molnupiravir) as a COVID treatment for vulnerable patients recently diagnosed with the disease.[^cite_note-274]
